   102	function cmdDoctor(args) {
   103	  const d = detect();
   104	  if (args.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(d, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
   105	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEWER DOCTOR');
   106	  console.log('='.repeat(60));
   107	  for (const [name, r] of Object.entries(d)) {
   108	    console.log(`${r.status.padEnd(12)} ${name}  (${r.role})`);
   109	    console.log(`             ${r.bin}`);
   110	    console.log(`             ${r.detail}`);
   111	  }
   112	  console.log('');
   113	  console.log('AVAILABLE means the binary exists and can be executed. It does NOT');
   114	  console.log('mean a review has been run, authenticated, or paid for.');
   115	  return EXIT_PASS;
   250	  };
   251	  if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || !parsed.disposition) {
   252	    return {
   253	      ...base,
   254	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   255	      unavailableReason: unavailableReason || 'the reviewer produced no parseable review record',
   256	      findings: [],
   257	    };
   258	  }
   259	  const allowed = ['APPROVE', 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES', 'REJECT'];
   260	  if (!allowed.includes(parsed.disposition)) {
   261	    return {
   262	      ...base,
   263	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   264	      unavailableReason: `the reviewer returned an unrecognised disposition ${JSON.stringify(parsed.disposition)}`,
   265	      findings: [],
   266	    };
   267	  }
   268	  const findings = Array.isArray(parsed.findings) ? parsed.findings : [];
   269	  return {
   270	    ...base,
   271	    disposition: parsed.disposition,
   272	    findings: findings
   273	      // A finding with empty evidence is dropped rather than passed through to
   274	      // fail schema validation and take the whole record down with it. Dropping
   275	      // an unevidenced finding is safe; dropping an evidenced one never happens.
   276	      .filter((f) => f && typeof f.evidence === 'string' && f.evidence.trim() !== '')
   277	      .map((f) => ({
   278	        severity: f.severity || 'INFORMATIONAL',
   279	        file: f.file || '(unspecified)',
   280	        location: f.location || '',
   281	        problem: f.problem || '',
   282	        evidence: f.evidence,
   283	        impact: f.impact || '',
   284	        requiredCorrection: f.requiredCorrection || '',
   285	        verificationMethod: f.verificationMethod || '',
   286	        status: 'OPEN',
   287	      })),
   288	    unverified: Array.isArray(parsed.unverified) ? parsed.unverified.map(String) : [],
   289	  };
   290	}
   291	
   292	function validateRecord(recordPath) {
   293	  const r = spawnSync('node', [ENGOS, '--validate-review', recordPath, '--json'],
   294	    { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   295	  let parsed = [];
   296	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* fall through */ }
   297	  const first = parsed[0] || { ok: false, errors: ['validator produced no result'] };
   298	  return { ok: !!first.ok, errors: first.errors || [] };
   299	}
   300	
   301	// ── run ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   302	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec) {
   303	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   304	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   305	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   306	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   307	  }
   308	  let argv;
   309	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   310	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   311	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   312	  } else {
   313	    // single-turn print mode; no approval flags, so no tool write can be accepted.
   314	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   315	  }
   316	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   317	    encoding: 'utf8',
   318	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   319	    maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
   320	    cwd: ROOT,
   321	  });
   322	  const raw = (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr ? `\n--- stderr ---\n${r.stderr}` : '');
   323	  if (r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') {
   324	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exceeded the ${timeoutSec}s timeout`, raw };
   325	  }
   326	  if (r.error) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} failed to start: ${r.error.message}`, raw };
   327	  if (r.status !== 0) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exited ${r.status}`, raw };
   328	  return { ok: true, raw };
   329	}
   330	
   331	function cmdRun(args) {
   332	  const reviewer = args.reviewer;
   333	  if (!reviewer) return usage('--reviewer is required');
   334	  if (!['codex', 'grok', 'copilot'].includes(reviewer)) return usage(`unknown reviewer ${reviewer}`);
   335	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   336	  if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) return usage(`packet not found: ${args.packet}`);
   337	
   338	  const packet = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8'));
   339	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   340	
   341	  if (args.subjectSha && args.subjectSha !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   342	    console.error(`[review-adapters] refusing to review: --subject-sha ${args.subjectSha.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the current subject ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…`);
   343	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   344	  }
   345	
   346	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   347	  fs.mkdirSync(RAW_DIR, { recursive: true });
   348	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   349	  const stamp = ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14);
   350	
   351	  // Copilot has no local binary and no read-only path that avoids mutating a
   352	  // PR. During this pass GitHub mutation is forbidden outright, so the only
   353	  // honest output is UNAVAILABLE with the reason stated.
   354	  if (reviewer === 'copilot') {
   355	    const d = detect().copilot;
   356	    const record = buildRecord({
   357	      reviewer: 'copilot',
   358	      reviewerModel: 'github-copilot-code-review',
   359	      packetId: packet.packetId,
   360	      subject,
   361	      parsed: null,
   362	      unavailableReason:
   363	        `Copilot review could not be collected. ${d.detail}. Requesting a review requires an open pull request and a GitHub API mutation, which this correction pass forbids.`,
   364	      ts,
   365	    });
   366	    return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-copilot`, '(no tool invoked — Copilot requires an open PR)');
   367	  }
   368	
   369	  const diff = subjectDiff(subject, args);
   370	  const prompt = reviewer === 'codex'
   371	    ? codexPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff)
   372	    : grokPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff);
   373	
   374	  if (args.dryRun) {
   375	    console.log(`[review-adapters] DRY RUN — ${reviewer}: would send ${prompt.length} chars over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} subject path(s). No tool invoked.`);
   376	    return EXIT_PASS;
   377	  }
   378	
   379	  const timeoutSec = Number(args.timeout) > 0 ? Number(args.timeout) : 900;
   380	  const res = runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec);
   381	  const rawPath = path.join(RAW_DIR, `${stamp}-${reviewer}.txt`);
   382	  fs.writeFileSync(rawPath, res.raw || '(no output captured)', 'utf8');
   383	
   384	  const parsed = res.ok ? extractJson(res.raw) : null;
   385	  const record = buildRecord({
   386	    reviewer,
   387	    reviewerModel: reviewer === 'codex' ? 'codex-cli (ChatGPT.app)' : 'grok-cli (grok-macos-aarch64)',
   388	    packetId: packet.packetId,
   389	    subject,
   390	    parsed,
   391	    unavailableReason: res.ok
   392	      ? 'the reviewer ran but produced no parseable JSON review record'
   393	      : res.reason,
   394	    ts,
   395	  });
   396	  return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-${reviewer}`, rawPath);
   397	}
   398	
   399	function writeRecord(record, base, rawPath) {
   400	  const outPath = path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, `${base}.json`);
   401	  fs.writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(record, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   402	  const v = validateRecord(outPath);
   403	  console.log(`[review-adapters] reviewer   : ${record.reviewer} (${record.reviewerModel})`);
   404	  console.log(`[review-adapters] disposition: ${record.disposition}`);
   405	  if (record.unavailableReason) console.log(`[review-adapters] reason     : ${record.unavailableReason}`);
   406	  console.log(`[review-adapters] findings   : ${(record.findings || []).length}`);
   407	  console.log(`[review-adapters] raw output : ${rawPath}`);
   408	  console.log(`[review-adapters] record     : ${path.relative(ROOT, outPath)}`);
   409	  if (!v.ok) {
   410	    console.error('[review-adapters] the produced record FAILED schema validation:');
   411	    for (const e of v.errors) console.error(`    ${e}`);
   412	    console.error('[review-adapters] the record is left on disk for inspection. The gate will refuse it.');
   413	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   414	  }
   415	  console.log('[review-adapters] record validates against engineering-review.schema.json');
   416	  return EXIT_PASS;
   417	}
   418	
   419	function usage(msg) {
   420	  if (msg) process.stderr.write(`\n[review-adapters] ${msg}\n`);
   421	  process.stderr.write(`
   422	review-adapters.cjs — read-only reviewer bridges
   423	
   424	  --doctor [--json]
   425	        Which reviewers are actually installed, at which absolute path.
   426	
   427	  --run --reviewer codex|grok|copilot --packet <p>
   428	        [--subject-sha <sha>] [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>]
   429	        [--timeout <seconds>] [--dry-run]
   430	        Run a reviewer read-only against the bound subject diff, preserve raw
   431	        output under review-raw/, and write a validated record under reviews/.
   432	
   433	No path in this file emits APPROVE unless a reviewer actually said so.
   434	`);
   435	  return EXIT_USAGE;
   436	}
   437	
   438	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   439	function parseArgs(argv) {
   440	  const a = {};
   441	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   442	    const t = argv[i];
   443	    if (t === '--reviewer') a.reviewer = argv[++i];
   444	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   445	    else if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   446	    else if (t === '--base') a.base = argv[++i];
   447	    else if (t === '--head') a.head = argv[++i];
   448	    else if (t === '--timeout') a.timeout = argv[++i];
   449	    else if (t === '--dry-run') a.dryRun = true;
   450	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   451	    else if (t === '--doctor') a.doctor = true;
   452	    else if (t === '--run') a.run = true;
   453	  }
   454	  return a;
   455	}
    20	    else if (a === '--data-class') out.dataClass = argv[++i];
    21	    else if (a === '--allow-metered') out.allowMetered = true;
    22	    else if (a === '--json') out.json = true;
    23	    else if (a === '--validate') out.validate = true;
    24	    else if (a === '--list') out.list = true;
    25	    else out.unknown = a;
    26	  }
    27	  return out;
    28	}
    29	
    30	function readCanon(file) {
    31	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));
    32	}
    33	
    34	function validate(canon) {
    35	  const errors = [];
    36	  if (!canon || typeof canon !== 'object') return ['canon must be an object'];
    37	  if (!Array.isArray(canon.taskTypes) || canon.taskTypes.length === 0) errors.push('taskTypes must be non-empty');
    38	  if (!Array.isArray(canon.tools) || canon.tools.length === 0) errors.push('tools must be non-empty');
    39	  if (canon.defaultMeteredBudgetUsd !== 0) errors.push('defaultMeteredBudgetUsd must remain 0; metered spend requires explicit authorization');
    40	
    41	  const taskIds = new Set();
    42	  const toolIds = new Set();
    43	  for (const task of canon.taskTypes || []) {
    44	    if (!task.taskId) errors.push('task missing taskId');
    45	    else if (taskIds.has(task.taskId)) errors.push(`duplicate taskId: ${task.taskId}`);
    46	    else taskIds.add(task.taskId);
    47	    if (!Array.isArray(task.preferredToolIds) || task.preferredToolIds.length === 0) errors.push(`${task.taskId || '(unknown task)'} has no preferredToolIds`);
    48	  }
    49	  for (const tool of canon.tools || []) {
    50	    if (!tool.toolId) errors.push('tool missing toolId');
    51	    else if (toolIds.has(tool.toolId)) errors.push(`duplicate toolId: ${tool.toolId}`);
    52	    else toolIds.add(tool.toolId);
    53	    if (!tool.availabilityEvidence || !tool.availabilityEvidence.result) errors.push(`${tool.toolId || '(unknown tool)'} has no availability evidence`);
    54	    for (const taskId of tool.taskIds || []) if (!taskIds.has(taskId)) errors.push(`${tool.toolId} references unknown taskId ${taskId}`);
    55	  }
    56	  for (const task of canon.taskTypes || []) {
    57	    for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds || []) if (!toolIds.has(toolId)) errors.push(`${task.taskId} prefers unknown tool ${toolId}`);
    58	  }
    59	  return errors;
    60	}
    61	
    62	function route(canon, opts) {
    63	  const task = canon.taskTypes.find((t) => t.taskId === opts.task);
    64	  if (!task) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_TASK', reason: `Task ${opts.task} is not in the Tool Capability Canon.` };
    65	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
    66	  if (!(dataClass in DATA_RANK)) return { ok: false, code: 'INVALID_DATA_CLASS', reason: `Unknown data class ${dataClass}.` };
    67	
    68	  const byId = new Map(canon.tools.map((t) => [t.toolId, t]));
    69	  const considered = [];
    70	  for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds) {
    71	    const tool = byId.get(toolId);
    72	    if (!tool) continue;
    73	    const reasons = [];
    74	    if (!tool.enabled || tool.availability === 'DISABLED') reasons.push('disabled');
    75	    if (tool.availability !== 'AVAILABLE') reasons.push(`availability=${tool.availability}`);
    76	    if (DATA_RANK[dataClass] > DATA_RANK[tool.maxDataClassification]) reasons.push(`data class ${dataClass} exceeds ${tool.maxDataClassification}`);
    77	    if (tool.costClass === 'METERED' && !opts.allowMetered) reasons.push('metered execution has no explicit budget authorization');
    78	    considered.push({ toolId, eligible: reasons.length === 0, reasons });
    79	    if (reasons.length === 0) {
    80	      return {
    81	        ok: true,
    82	        taskId: task.taskId,
    83	        toolId: tool.toolId,
    84	        label: tool.label,
    85	        executionMode: tool.executionModes[0],
    86	        costClass: tool.costClass,
    87	        dataClass,
    88	        verification: tool.verification,
    89	        requiredInputs: task.requiredInputs || [],
    90	        requiredOutputs: task.requiredOutputs || [],
    91	        considered,
    92	      };
    93	    }
    94	  }
    95	  return {
    96	    ok: false,
    97	    code: 'SPECIALIST_REQUIRED',
    98	    reason: `No verified, enabled specialist can execute ${task.taskId}. Generalist fallback is ${task.generalistFallback}.`,
    99	    taskId: task.taskId,
   100	    considered,
   101	  };
   102	}
   103	
   104	function main() {
   105	  const opts = parse(process.argv.slice(2));
   106	  if (opts.unknown) {
   107	    console.error(`Unknown argument: ${opts.unknown}`);
   108	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   109	  }
   110	  const canonPath = path.resolve(ROOT, opts.canon || path.relative(ROOT, DEFAULT_CANON));
   111	  let canon;
   112	  try { canon = readCanon(canonPath); }
   113	  catch (e) { console.error(`Cannot read canon ${canonPath}: ${e.message}`); return EXIT_USAGE; }
   114	  const errors = validate(canon);
   115	  if (errors.length) {
   116	    console.error('TOOL CANON INVALID');
   117	    for (const error of errors) console.error(`- ${error}`);
   118	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   119	  }
   120	  if (opts.validate) {
   121	    console.log(`TOOL CANON VALID — ${canon.taskTypes.length} task type(s), ${canon.tools.length} tool profile(s), default metered budget $${canon.defaultMeteredBudgetUsd}`);
   122	    return EXIT_OK;
   123	  }
   124	  if (opts.list) {
   125	    for (const task of canon.taskTypes) console.log(`${task.taskId}: ${task.preferredToolIds.join(', ')}; fallback=${task.generalistFallback}`);

    74	function auditSource() {
    75	  return {
    76	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, d.p) })),
    77	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, p) })),
    78	  };
    79	}

    81	function auditTarget(target) {
    82	  return {
    83	    isDir: fs.existsSync(target) && fs.statSync(target).isDirectory(),
    84	    isGit: fs.existsSync(path.join(target, '.git')),
    85	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(target, d.p) })),
    86	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(target, p) })),
    87	  };
    88	}

   198	function parseArgs(argv) {
   199	  const a = {};
   200	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   201	    const t = argv[i];
   202	    if (t === '--target') a.target = argv[++i];
   203	    else if (t === '--install') a.install = true;
   204	    else if (t === '--doctor') a.doctor = true;
   205	    else if (t === '--force') a.force = true;
   206	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   207	  }
   208	  return a;
   209	}

   160	// ── reviewers (from the tool canon — availability with dated evidence) ──────
   161	function projectReviewers(now) {
   162	  if (!fs.existsSync(CANON)) {
   163	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon not found at ${rel(CANON)}`, reviewers: [] };
   164	  }
   165	  let canon;
   166	  try { canon = readJSON(CANON); }
   167	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}`, reviewers: [] }; }
   168	
   169	  const wanted = { 'codex-local': 'independent review', 'grok-cli': 'adversarial red team' };
   170	  const reviewers = [];
   171	  for (const [toolId, role] of Object.entries(wanted)) {
   172	    const t = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === toolId);
   173	    if (!t) {
   174	      reviewers.push({ toolId, role, availability: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   175	        reason: 'not present in the Tool Capability Canon', evidence: null, source: rel(CANON) });
   176	      continue;
   177	    }
   178	    const ev = t.availabilityEvidence || null;
   179	    reviewers.push({
   180	      toolId, role,
   181	      label: t.label || toolId,
   182	      availability: t.availability || 'UNKNOWN',
   183	      enabled: t.enabled !== false,
   184	      evidence: ev ? { observedAt: ev.observedAt, method: ev.method, result: ev.result } : null,
   185	      evidenceAgeMinutes: ev && ev.observedAt ? ageMinutes(ev.observedAt, now) : null,
   186	      source: rel(CANON),
   187	    });
   188	  }
   189	  return { state: 'OK', reviewers };
   190	}
   191	
   192	// ── engineering state (the gate's own verdict — never re-judged here) ───────
   193	function projectEngineering(args) {
   194	  const a = [ENGOS, '--gate-done', '--json'];
   195	  if (args.packet) a.push('--packet', args.packet);
   196	  if (args.subjectSha) a.push('--subject-sha', args.subjectSha);
   197	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   198	
   199	  // A BLOCKED gate exits 3. That is a successful reading of a blocked system,
   200	  // not a failure to read — so it is projected, not swallowed.
   201	  let parsed = null;
   202	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* handled below */ }
   203	  if (!parsed) {
   204	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   205	      reason: `could not read a gate verdict (exit ${r.status}): ${(r.stderr || '').trim().split('\n')[0] || 'no output'}`,
   206	      source: rel(ENGOS) };
   207	  }
   208	
   209	  const stageEvidence = deriveStages(parsed);
   210	  return {
   211	    state: 'OK',
   212	    gateExit: r.status,
   213	    verdict: parsed.ok ? parsed.state : 'BLOCKED',
   214	    lane: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : 'UNKNOWN',
   215	    highRisk: parsed.classification ? !!parsed.classification.highRisk : null,
   216	    requiredReviewers: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.requiredReviewers : [],
   217	    subjectSha256: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectSha256 : null,
   218	    subjectPaths: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectPaths : [],
   219	    excludedAsEvidence: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.excludedAsEvidence : [],
   220	    problems: parsed.problems || [],
   221	    observed: parsed.observed || [],
   222	    unverified: parsed.unverified || [],
   223	    stages: stageEvidence,
   224	    source: rel(ENGOS),
   225	  };
   226	}
   227	
   228	// Map the gate verdict onto the declared stage sequence. This does not decide
   229	// anything — it re-expresses the gate's own problems as stage states so the
   230	// drift is drawable. A stage with no positive evidence is UNVERIFIED, which is
   231	// distinct from FAILED: nobody looked, versus somebody looked and it failed.
   232	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   233	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   234	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   235	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   236	
   237	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   238	    switch (id) {
   239	      case 'spec':
   240	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   241	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   242	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   243	      case 'build':
   244	        return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'a subject diff exists to review' };
   245	      case 'deterministic':
   246	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   247	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   248	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   249	      case 'codex':
   250	      case 'grok': {
   251	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   252	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   253	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   254	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   255	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   256	        return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'an approval is bound to this subject' };
   257	      }
   258	      case 'copilot':
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   260	      case 'gate':
   261	        return parsed.ok
   262	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   263	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   264	      case 'runtime':
   265	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   266	      case 'checkpoint':
   267	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   268	      default:
   269	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   270	    }
   271	  };
   272	
   273	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   274	}
   275	
   310	    conn.connectors.forEach(function(c){

   348	      })[0];
   349	      if (st) right.appendChild(chip(st.state === 'PASS' ? 'PASS' : st.state));
   350	      li.appendChild(left); li.appendChild(right);
   351	      li.tabIndex = 0; li.style.cursor='pointer';

    70	## 4. Connector abstraction
    71	
    72	Every connector declares which operations it supports — no operation is assumed:
    73	
    74	`CONNECT · DISCONNECT · HEALTH_CHECK · READ · WRITE · SEARCH · CREATE · UPDATE ·
    75	DELETE · TRIGGER · STATUS`
    76	
    77	and reports: `status · authStatus · lastHealthCheck · lastSuccess · lastFailure ·
    78	failureCount · latencyMs · rateLimit · permissions · dependentWorkflows ·
    79	environment · riskLevel`.
    80	
    81	Health vocabulary: `HEALTHY · DEGRADED · FAILED · DISCONNECTED · AUTH_EXPIRED ·
    82	RATE_LIMITED · UNKNOWN`. **`UNKNOWN` is the default** — a connector that has
    83	never been probed is UNKNOWN, never HEALTHY. Optimism by default is how an
    84	outage stays invisible.
    85	
    86	## 5. Integration router — preference order
    87	
    88	For a required external action, choose the **smallest reliable path**:
    89	
    90	1. **Direct API** — preferred for a simple, reliable, single-system action.
    91	2. **Built-in / plugin / MCP** — when already authenticated and in-process.
    92	3. **Automation platform (Make)** — only for genuinely multi-system business
    93	   workflows.
    94	4. **n8n — LEGACY/OPTIONAL.** Not a default substrate. It is dead in this
    95	   estate (zero completed jobs since 2026-05-18) and must never be assumed live.
    96	
    97	Selection inputs: capability · availability with evidence · permissions ·
    98	historical failure rate · latency · security/data class · cost.
    99	
   100	**Never route a simple direct API call through an automation chain.** Each hop
   101	is a place the action can fail silently and a place the truth can be laundered.
   102	
   103	## 6. Failure isolation
   104	
   105	Engineering state and integration state are separate fields, always:
   106	
   107	```
   108	ENGINEERING STATUS : READY_FOR_PR      (control plane)
   109	DOCUMENTATION SYNC : FAILED            (integration plane)
   110	CONNECTOR          : notion
   111	RETRY              : AVAILABLE
   112	```
   113	
   114	A failed connector **never** downgrades verified engineering. A failed sync is
   115	**never** rendered as successful. Two states, two fields, no blending.
   116	
   117	## 7. Idempotency
   118	
   119	Every meaningful external operation carries `operationId` (deterministic from
   120	objective + workflow + target), `correlationId`, `attempt`, `ts`, `result`.
   121	A retry with the same `operationId` is a no-op at the target. Destructive
   122	operations are never blindly repeated.
   123	
   124	## 8. Events
   125	
   126	Connector and workflow events are **ledger entries**, not a new store:
   127	`gate: "integration"`, `status: PASS|BLOCKED|FAILED`, plus operation ids.
   128	This inherits append-only semantics, the existing schema, and the existing
   129	audit tooling for free.
   130	
   131	## 9. Live topology + drift visualisation
   132	
   133	Nodes render only for systems **actually configured**. Node state is derived
   134	from real sources and may be: `PENDING · ACTIVE · PASS · WARNING · FAILED ·
   135	BLOCKED · WAITING_FOR_HUMAN · UNAVAILABLE · DEGRADED · UNKNOWN`.

    10	
    11	AEGIS gives the Product Owner control over AI-assisted builds without requiring
    12	him to read long agent transcripts. Within roughly ten seconds he should know:
    13	
    14	- what is being built and against which approved requirement;
    15	- the current workflow state, agent, model, tool, and execution mode;
    16	- what failed, what is blocked, and what needs a human decision;
    17	- which checks and reviews actually ran and where their evidence lives;
    18	- whether metered spending occurred;
    19	- the last known-good checkpoint and available rollback action.
    20	
    21	The dashboard observes and controls the canonical Workflow Engine. **It does
    22	not own or duplicate workflow state.**
    23	
    24	## 2. Local-first boundary
    25	
    26	The following live on Marc's machine in V1:
    27	
    28	- dashboard application and local control service;
    29	- durable workflow, policy, event, approval, and evidence records;
    30	- SQLite database in WAL mode;
    31	- repositories, isolated worktrees, specifications, and local artifacts;
    32	- watchdog, heartbeats, leases, stale-run detection, and recovery metadata;
    33	- tool/model configuration and budget policy;
    34	- credential references. Secret values belong in macOS Keychain, never the DB,
    35	  prompts, event log, screenshots, or repository.
    36	
    37	GitHub, Notion, and cloud AI/media tools remain external services. Local-first
    38	does not mean local inference: relevant context may leave the machine when an
    39	external provider is used. The router must enforce data-classification limits
    40	and record the provider selected.
    41	
    42	## 3. Durable control plane
    43	
    44	The orchestrator does not remember the state. A durable state machine owns it.
    45	Every transition records actor, timestamp, subject, evidence, and policy
    46	decision. Agents request transitions; policy permits or refuses them.
    47	
    48	Minimum states:
    49	
    50	`PENDING -> ANALYZING -> PLANNED -> IMPLEMENTING -> DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION ->
    51	INDEPENDENT_REVIEW -> CORRECTION -> RUNTIME_VALIDATION -> CHECKPOINTING ->
    52	VERIFIED`
    53	
    54	`BLOCKED`, `PAUSED`, `CANCELLED`, and `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` are explicit states.
    55	Retries are idempotent. A lease prevents two workers owning one step. A
    56	heartbeat and timeout expose abandoned work. Resume starts from durable state,
    57	not conversation memory.
    58	
    59	## 4. Execution modes and money
    60	
    61	Every executable capability declares one mode:
    62	
    63	1. `SUBSCRIPTION_CLI` — preferred when a supported authenticated CLI uses an
    64	   existing subscription allowance.
    65	2. `BUILT_IN` or `PLUGIN_MCP` — preferred when already available in the active
    66	   surface and suitable for the task.
    67	3. `DESKTOP_ASSISTED` — for attended or semi-attended work such as documents,
    68	   Notion, or a provider UI. It is not trusted as the only unattended control
    69	   path.
    70	4. `API_METERED` — reliable headless execution when authorized.
    71	5. `UNAVAILABLE` — honest terminal state when nothing appropriate can run.
    72	
    73	Default metered budget is **$0**. A non-zero metered run requires a named human
    74	approval and a hard cap. Included-plan usage is still tracked because plan
    75	limits are finite.
    76	
    77	## 5. Capability-first tool routing
    78	
    79	AEGIS routes the task to a verified capability before choosing a model.
    80	
    81	- Claude is not a video generator, advertising platform, design application,
    82	  deployment host, or spreadsheet engine merely because it can describe one.
    83	- A specialist-required task cannot fall back silently to a general model.
    84	- Video generation prefers OpenArt when its connector is verified and enabled.
    85	- Advertising requires an enabled advertising specialist selected in the Tool
    86	  Capability Canon. Until one is connected, advertising execution blocks.
    87	- Attachments, MCPs, plugins, APIs, CLIs, desktop tools, and future add-ons are
    88	  all connector types behind the same selection contract.
    89	- Tools may be enabled, disabled, reprioritized, or replaced without rewriting
    90	  workflow doctrine.
    91	
    92	Selection considers task match, availability evidence, data classification,
    93	cost authorization, required inputs, output type, and owner preference. If no
    94	eligible specialist exists, return `SPECIALIST_REQUIRED`, not an improvised
    95	output.
    96	
    97	## 6. Lean workflow
    98	
    99	The ordering rule is **cheapest reliable falsifier first**:
   100	
   101	`objective -> pinned acceptance criteria -> risk -> isolated worktree -> build
   102	-> syntax/type/lint/unit/build checks -> independent review -> bounded
   103	correction -> checks again -> PR/CI -> repository review -> runtime evidence ->
   104	risk-gated red team -> watchdog -> checkpoint -> verified documentation sync`
   105	
   106	Grok is required for high-risk, novel, security-sensitive, control-system, and
   107	milestone work. It is not called for harmless formatting or tiny documentation
   108	changes. Maximum normal correction cycles: three.
   109	
   110	## 7. Tool Capability Canon
   111	
   112	`builder-control/TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json` is the versioned seed catalog for
   113	task-to-tool policy. The live dashboard will store current probe results in its
   114	local DB, but may not silently rewrite the canon.
   115	
   116	Each tool profile names:
   117	
   118	- supported task types and capabilities;
   119	- preferred execution modes;
   120	- availability and evidence;
   121	- strengths and explicit exclusions;
   122	- required inputs and output artifacts;
   123	- cost class, approval rule, and data-classification ceiling;
   124	- verification required before its output is accepted.
   125	
   126	## 8. V1 vertical slice
   127	
   128	V1 proves one complete local loop:
   129	
   130	1. text objective entered;
   131	2. acceptance criteria and risk recorded;
   132	3. tool/model route selected from verified capabilities;
   133	4. isolated worktree created;
   134	5. builder runs;
   135	6. deterministic checks run;
   136	7. independent review binds to the exact subject;
   137	8. corrections are bounded and rechecked;
   138	9. watchdog proves the policy sequence;
   139	10. checkpoint and rollback point recorded;
   140	11. dashboard displays evidence, costs, blockers, and controls.
   141	
   142	Notion begins as approved-intake plus one-way post-checkpoint synchronization.
   143	Bi-directional conflict resolution, voice, cinematic visualization, historical
   144	model learning, and dynamic optimization are later versions.
   145	
   146	## 9. Anti-theatre acceptance
   147	
   148	AEGIS V1 is not complete until the vertical slice is executed end-to-end and a
   149	deliberate negative case proves each control blocks: missing specialist, stale
   150	review, unavailable builder, failed deterministic check, skipped workflow step,

   574	
   575	// Rules the JSON Schema cannot express, but which decide whether a record is
   576	// honest evidence rather than a well-formed shell.
   577	function reviewSemantics(rec) {
   578	  const errs = [];
   579	  if (rec.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE' && !rec.unavailableReason) {
   580	    errs.push('disposition UNAVAILABLE requires unavailableReason — "it did not run" must say why');
   581	  }
   582	  if (rec.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE' && (rec.findings || []).length) {
   583	    errs.push('disposition UNAVAILABLE cannot carry findings — a reviewer that did not run cannot have found anything');
   584	  }
   585	  // A record must say WHAT it read. Without changedPaths there is no way to
   586	  // tell a full review from one that glanced at a single file.
   587	  if (!rec.reviewOf || !Array.isArray(rec.reviewOf.changedPaths) || rec.reviewOf.changedPaths.length === 0) {
   588	    if (rec.disposition !== 'UNAVAILABLE') {
   589	      errs.push('reviewOf.changedPaths is required and must be non-empty — a review that cannot say what it read is not evidence');
   590	    }
   591	  }
   592	  for (const [i, f] of (rec.findings || []).entries()) {
   593	    if (typeof f.evidence === 'string' && f.evidence.trim() === '') {
   594	      errs.push(`findings[${i}]: evidence is empty — a finding with no evidence is an opinion`);
   595	    }
   596	    if (f.status === 'DISPUTED' && !f.builderResponse) errs.push(`findings[${i}]: status DISPUTED requires builderResponse`);
   597	    if (f.severity === 'FALSE_POSITIVE' && !f.builderResponse) errs.push(`findings[${i}]: severity FALSE_POSITIVE requires builderResponse`);
   598	    if (f.status === 'ACCEPTED_RISK' && !f.acceptedBy) errs.push(`findings[${i}]: status ACCEPTED_RISK requires acceptedBy (a human — an AI may not accept risk for the Product Owner)`);
   599	    // A CRITICAL/HIGH finding without impact, correction and verification is
   600	    // an alarm with no instructions: it blocks the build and tells nobody how
   601	    // to clear it.
   602	    if (f.severity === 'CRITICAL' || f.severity === 'HIGH') {
   603	      for (const req of ['impact', 'requiredCorrection', 'verificationMethod']) {
   604	        if (!f[req] || String(f[req]).trim() === '') {
   605	          errs.push(`findings[${i}]: ${f.severity} findings require a non-empty ${req}`);
   606	        }
   607	      }
   608	    }
   609	    // FIXED is a claim about work done AFTER the finding was raised. The
   610	    // builder asserting its own fix is exactly the self-approval this system
   611	    // exists to prevent, so FIXED must point at the reviewer record that
   612	    // re-checked it.
   613	    if (f.status === 'FIXED' && !f.verifiedByReviewId) {
   614	      errs.push(`findings[${i}]: status FIXED requires verifiedByReviewId naming a reviewer record that re-verified it. A builder may not mark its own finding fixed.`);
   615	    }
   616	  }
   617	  return errs;
   618	}
   619	
   620	function loadReview(p) {
   621	  if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return { path: p, ok: false, errors: [`file not found: ${p}`] };
   622	  let rec;
   623	  try { rec = readJSON(p); } catch (e) { return { path: p, ok: false, errors: [`not valid JSON: ${e.message}`] }; }
   624	  const schema = readJSON(REVIEW_SCHEMA);
   625	  const errors = [...validateAgainst(schema, rec), ...reviewSemantics(rec)];
   626	  return { path: p, ok: errors.length === 0, errors, rec };
   627	}
   628	
   629	function cmdValidateReview(args) {
   630	  const files = args.review.length ? args.review : args._;
   631	  if (!files.length) die('usage: --validate-review <review.json> [more.json ...]', EXIT_USAGE);
   632	  let bad = 0;
   633	  const results = files.map(loadReview);
   634	  if (args.json) {
   635	    out(JSON.stringify(results.map((r) => ({ path: r.path, ok: r.ok, errors: r.errors })), null, 2));
   636	    return results.every((r) => r.ok) ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_USAGE;
   637	  }
   638	  out('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEW RECORD VALIDATION');
   639	  out('='.repeat(52));
   640	  for (const r of results) {
   641	    if (r.ok) { out(`ok    ${r.path}  (${r.rec.reviewer} / ${r.rec.disposition})`); }
   642	    else { bad++; out(`FAIL  ${r.path}`); for (const e of r.errors) out(`        ${e}`); }
   643	  }
   644	  out('');
   645	  out(bad ? `${bad} invalid record(s).` : `All ${results.length} record(s) valid.`);
   646	  return bad ? EXIT_USAGE : EXIT_PASS;
   647	}
   648	
   649	// Gather review records from explicit --review args and from the conventional
   650	// evidence directory, so CI does not have to enumerate them.
   651	function collectReviewPaths(args) {
   652	  const explicit = args.review.slice();
   653	  const dir = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
   654	  const fromDir = fs.existsSync(dir)
   655	    ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json')).map((f) => path.join(dir, f))
   656	    : [];
   657	  return [...new Set([...explicit, ...fromDir])];
   658	}
   659	
   660	// ── COMMAND: --gate-done ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   661	// The fail-closed question: given what actually exists on disk, has this change
   662	// earned the right to move forward?
   663	//
   664	// Terminal states are deliberately NOT "DONE". Reviews existing is not the same
   665	// as software being correct, and a word that implies finished invites everyone
   666	// downstream to stop checking.
   667	const STATE_READY_DET = 'READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION';
   668	const STATE_READY_PR = 'READY_FOR_PR';
   669	
   670	function gateDone(args) {
   671	  const problems = [];
   672	  const observed = [];
   673	
   674	  // 1. Subject binding is MANDATORY and always enforced. Without it there is
   675	  //    no answer to "reviewed WHAT?", and every downstream check is decoration.
   676	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
   677	  const claimed = args.subjectSha || args.diffSha || null;
   678	  if (!claimed) {
   679	    problems.push({
   680	      rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING',
   681	      detail: 'no --subject-sha was supplied. A gate decision with no bound subject certifies nothing. Compute it with `--subject --json` and pass it back.',
   682	    });
   683	  } else if (!/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(claimed)) {
   684	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING', detail: `--subject-sha ${JSON.stringify(claimed)} is not a sha256 hex digest` });
   685	  } else if (claimed !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   686	    problems.push({
   687	      rule: 'ENGOS-SUBJECT-MISMATCH',
   688	      detail: `--subject-sha ${claimed.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the subject computed here (${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…). The tree moved, or the wrong subject was supplied.`,
   689	    });
   690	  } else {
   691	    observed.push(`subject bound: ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s)`);
   692	  }
   693	  if (subject.excludedAsEvidence.length) {
   694	    observed.push(`${subject.excludedAsEvidence.length} evidence path(s) excluded from the subject — adding review records does not move this hash`);
   695	  }
   696	
   697	  // 2. Lane, computed from the SUBJECT only.
   698	  const cls = classify(subject.subjectPaths, { milestone: args.milestone, novel: args.novel, diffLines: args.diffLines });
   699	  observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? ' (high-risk)' : ''}; required reviewers: ${cls.requiredReviewers.join(', ') || 'none'}`);
   700	
   701	  // 3. Packet: required for every lane except LIGHT.
   702	  let packetId = null;
   703	  if (cls.requiresPacket) {
   704	    if (!args.packet) {
   705	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `lane ${cls.lane} requires a task packet; none was supplied` });
   706	    } else if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) {
   707	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `packet not found: ${args.packet}` });
   708	    } else {
   709	      const pv = spawnSync('node', [PACKET_TOOLS, '--validate', args.packet], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   710	      if (pv.status !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-INVALID', detail: `packet-tools rejected the packet (exit ${pv.status}). Fix the packet first.` });
   711	      else observed.push('task packet is valid (packet-tools.cjs)');
   712	      try { packetId = readJSON(args.packet).packetId; } catch { /* reported above */ }
   713	
   714	      const sp = specCheck(args.packet);
   715	      if (!sp.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED', detail: `${sp.unresolved.length} source(s) of truth are neither pinnable nor marked UNVERIFIED: ${sp.unresolved.map((r) => r.source).join(', ')}` });
   716	      else observed.push(`spec pin: ${sp.rows.length - sp.unverified.length} pinned, ${sp.unverified.length} explicitly UNVERIFIED`);
   717	      var specRows = sp;
   718	    }
   719	  } else {
   720	    observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}: no task packet required — deterministic checks are the whole gate here`);
   721	  }
   722	
   723	  // 4. Load records. Malformed records are errors; they are never skipped.
   724	  const loaded = collectReviewPaths(args).map(loadReview);
   725	  for (const l of loaded) if (!l.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MALFORMED', detail: `${l.path}: ${l.errors.join('; ')}` });
    25	    },

    35	function run(args) {
    36	  const r = spawnSync('node', [CLI, ...args], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
    37	  return { exit: r.status, out: (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr || '') };
    38	}
    39	
    40	function expect(name, args, { exit, contains, notContains }) {
    41	  const r = run(args);
    42	  const okExit = r.exit === exit;
    43	  const okHas = contains ? [].concat(contains).every((c) => r.out.includes(c)) : true;
    44	  const okNot = notContains ? ![].concat(notContains).some((c) => r.out.includes(c)) : true;
    45	  if (okExit && okHas && okNot) {
    46	    pass++;
    47	    console.log(`  ok   ${name}  (exit ${r.exit})`);
    48	  } else {
    49	    fail++;
    50	    console.error(`  FAIL ${name}`);
    51	    console.error(`       expected exit=${exit}${contains ? `, containing ${JSON.stringify([].concat(contains))}` : ''}`);
    52	    console.error(`       got exit=${r.exit}`);
    53	    console.error(r.out.split('\n').map((l) => '         ' + l).join('\n'));
    54	  }
    55	}

    57	function writeJSON(name, obj) {
    58	  const p = path.join(TMP, name);
    59	  fs.writeFileSync(p, JSON.stringify(obj, null, 2));
    60	  return p;
    61	}

   276	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   277	function projectEvents(limit) {
   278	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   279	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   280	  }
   281	  let l;
   282	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   283	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   284	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   285	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   286	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   287	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   288	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   289	    operation: e.operation || null,
   290	  }));
   291	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };
   292	}
   293	
   294	// ── snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   295	function snapshot(args) {
   296	  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();
   297	  const now = Date.parse(nowIso);
   298	  const engineering = projectEngineering(args);
   299	  const integration = { connectors: projectConnectors(now) };
   300	
   301	  // Failure isolation, made structural: these are two objects. There is no
   302	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   303	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   304	  return {
   305	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   306	    contract: {
   307	      absences: ['UNAVAILABLE', 'STALE', 'UNVERIFIED'],
   308	      note: 'Engineering and integration state are separate and are never blended. No field in this snapshot is synthesised; every value cites the artifact it came from.',
   309	    },
   310	    engineering,
   311	    integration,
   312	    reviewers: projectReviewers(now),
   313	    events: projectEvents(Number(args.events) > 0 ? Number(args.events) : 12),
   314	  };
   315	}
   316	
   317	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   318	function parseArgs(argv) {
   319	  const a = {};
   320	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   321	    const t = argv[i];
   322	    if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   323	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   324	    else if (t === '--events') a.events = argv[++i];
   325	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   326	    else if (t === '--out') a.out = argv[++i];
   327	  }
   328	  return a;
   329	}
   330	
   331	if (require.main === module) {
   332	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   333	  let snap;
   334	  try {
   335	    snap = snapshot(args);
   336	  } catch (e) {
   337	    if (e.refused) {
   338	      process.stderr.write(`\nAEGIS-STATE REFUSED\n  rule:   AEGIS-CONNECTOR-PLANE-VIOLATION\n  reason: ${e.message}\n`);
   339	      process.exit(EXIT_REFUSED);
   340	    }
   341	    throw e;
   342	  }
   343	  const text = JSON.stringify(snap, null, 2);
   344	  if (args.out) {
   345	    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(args.out), { recursive: true });
   346	    // A `.js` target emits a global assignment rather than bare JSON. The
   347	    // dashboard has to work from file:// with no server — that is the whole
   348	    // point of an evidence view — and fetch() of a local JSON file is blocked
   349	    // by the file:// origin policy. A script tag is not. The content is
   350	    // identical either way; only the wrapper differs.
   351	    const isJs = args.out.endsWith('.js');
   352	    const body = isJs
   353	      ? `/* Generated by builder-control/aegis-state.cjs — do not edit by hand.\n   Every value here cites the artifact it came from. Regenerate with:\n     node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js */\nwindow.AEGIS_STATE = ${text};\n`
   354	      : text + '\n';
   355	    fs.writeFileSync(args.out, body, 'utf8');
   356	    process.stderr.write(`[aegis-state] wrote ${rel(path.resolve(args.out))}\n`);
   357	  } else {
   358	    process.stdout.write(text + '\n');
   359	  }
   360	  process.exit(EXIT_PASS);
   361	}
